# Build-report queue — every item not yet ✅

The sixth absolute rule (CLAUDE.md) keeps this file. **Read it before starting any work of our own choosing.**
A row leaves this table only when the PR that resolves it is MERGED; its ledger stays in `PROGRESS.md`.
A row with a PR number in **Owner** is taken by that session.
**Picking a new ID:** several sessions add rows at once, and an open PR's rows are in no copy of this file you
can read. Take an ID above every ID in this file AND in the open PRs' diffs of it; `tests/theQueueIdsAreUnique.test.ts`
fails a merged state where two rows share one, and the session whose PR is not yet merged renumbers its own rows.

States: **OPEN** (actionable, nobody working it) · **IN PROGRESS** (owner set) · **🟡 BLOCKED** (all four fields:
what · why · needs · tried).

Seeded 2026-10-01 from the items the 6461025c and bee95692 autopsies left without a final state. Older
"OPEN root cause" entries in `PROGRESS.md` predate this file; move one here when it is next touched.

| Q-881 | Billing audit 2026-10-12 (F-02) | The live USD→INR rate (`UsdInrRate.ts`) is accepted from open.er-api.com without any sanity band and multiplies every bill on that instance | IN PROGRESS | this PR (`claude/q881-bound-the-fx-rate`) | **Fix:** absolute band + ±5% from last good value; keep last good on refusal; record the rate on every debit. **Why blocked:** the audit brief says wait for explicit approval before implementing. **Tried:** read the module; no band exists. 🔴 **STATE CORRECTED 2026-10-20 (Q-1126 batch 3): this was 🟡 BLOCKED — *awaiting approval of the audit plan* — and that is not a blocker. It is the THIRD row filed that way whose fix needs no admin decision (Q-886 and Q-888 were the first two, both now fixed in #3854 and #3857), so the mis-filing is a pattern, not an accident.** **VERIFIED LIVE 2026-10-20 at `src/server/lib/UsdInrRate.ts:50-62`, not taken from this row:** the entire validation on the rate that multiplies every bill is `Number.isFinite(rate) && rate > 0`. So any positive finite number `open.er-api.com` returns is accepted — `0.5`, `5000`, a glitch, a compromise — and becomes the multiplier on that instance’s billing until the next hourly refresh. **Why no decision is in it:** the stated fix (absolute band + ±5% from the last good value, keep the last good value on refusal) is STRICTLY SAFER than today, because today accepts anything positive; the band values are engineering judgement, and “keep last good on refusal” means it cannot break billing — the module’s own comment already says *“billing must never break on FX”*. ⚠️ **AND ITS THIRD CLAUSE IS DELIBERATELY NOT PART OF THIS:** *“record the rate on every debit”* touches the billing WRITE path and is a larger, separate change. The band is the safe, high-value half; bundling them is how a safe fix inherits a risky one’s blocker (the same reasoning that kept Q-1227 out of Q-886). That clause stays open after the band ships. ✅ **FIXED — AND THE SHAPE OF THE FIX IS THE WHOLE POINT, because the obvious one would have re-created a bug this module already had.** The band half is done: an absolute band `[30, 300]` refuses the impossible outright, deliberately WIDE (a 2.5x move from today’s ~85 still lands) because it exists to catch a broken payload, not to have an opinion about FX — a test asserts the band spans more than 5x so a future narrowing cannot quietly turn it into a forecast. 🔴 **AND A BIG JUMP IS HELD, NOT REFUSED.** The revenue audit of 2026-09-10 found `refreshUsdInrRate` was never wired outside its own test, so this rate sat FROZEN at the 85 fallback while the real rate was 87–88 and every build was under-billed ~3% in silence. A plain “refuse anything more than 5% from the last good value” band freezes it again the first time the rupee really moves 6% — the same bug with a safety badge on. So a move beyond 5% lands only when a second INDEPENDENT fetch agrees (itself a small move, not an exact match — the true rate drifts between observations): one glitch never bills anybody, and a genuine move is late by one cycle, never permanent. **Every refusal is LOUD with a running count**, and `usdInrRateHealth()` exposes the held candidate and the last reason — that 2026-09 bug was invisible for months precisely because a frozen rate looks exactly like a working one from outside. 🔑 **TWO SIBLINGS THIS ROW DOES NOT NAME, both found by hunting the class “a number that multiplies every bill, wherever it enters”:** `setUsdInrRate` (the ops override) carried the identical weak check, so a hand-typed `8500` was as acceptable as a glitched payload — banded now, though deliberately NOT jump-limited, since an override is a human act and making someone confirm their own keystroke twice just teaches them to call it twice; and the **env fallback** had it too, where a typo of `850` for `85` would have multiplied every bill by ten (CLAUDE.md’s own warning: an env value always beats a code default). **Locked by `tests/theRateThatMultipliesEveryBillIsBounded.test.ts` (11 tests) plus the module’s existing 5, unchanged; 4 reversions bite** (the any-positive check returns → 4 fail · a big jump refused outright, i.e. the frozen-rate bug re-created → 2 · the ops override loses its band → 1 · the refusal goes quiet after the first → 1). ⚠️ **STILL OPEN AFTER THIS, deliberately:** the row’s third clause, *“record the rate on every debit”*. That touches the billing WRITE path and is a separate, larger change; bundling is how a safe fix inherits a risky one’s blocker. This row stays OPEN for that clause once the band lands — it is NOT closed by this PR. |

| Q-979 | found gating Q-975 on main after #3692 (2026-10-13) | The `/build` route handler in `routes/agentv3.ts` sits at TypeScript's control-flow-analysis limit: `typecheck:server` fails with TS2563 ("too large for control flow analysis") as soon as a few more branches are added to it. It hit twice in one day (Q-978, then Q-975 on top of #3692) | OPEN | — | **Class:** one function that every build feature adds a branch to, with no budget. TS2563 trips when a reference's flow walk passes ~2000 nested branch joins, so it surfaces in whichever PR adds the last few `if`/`&&`/ternaries — a red CI that names a file the PR may not have touched. **Mitigated so far:** #3698's merge moved the leftover-net guard out (`applyLeftoverNetAfterBuild`); the Q-975 PR adds ZERO branches to the handler and moves one more out (its milestone conditions live in `milestoneBuildPrompt` / `milestoneLaneRequest`, and the stay record in `recordReasoningStays`). **Needs:** headroom, not a mass rewrite — move self-contained blocks (the finishing passes, the mega-roadmap planner block, the fast-lane setup) into modules one PR at a time, each gated by the full suite; until then, a PR touching the handler puts new conditions inside a module function. Not BLOCKED: actionable by any session, but large, so it is not started inside an unrelated PR.  🔴 **CORRECTION 2026-10-18, AND THE ROW NAMED THE WRONG FUNCTION.** This row says *"the `/build` route handler"*. **There is no `/build` route.** Measured across every `app.get`/`app.post` in the file: the handler at the limit is **`/api/agentv3/chat` — 17,595 raw lines (2,501 with comments stripped)**, against 444 for the next largest (`/publish`). All nine modules that carry a comment about this limit (`FastLaneBudget`, `thirdPartyLimits`, `gateRedNotice`, `handoffRule`, `finishingPassesAtCap`, `leftoverComponents`, `ProjectPlan`, …) say "the CHAT handler", so the row was the odd one out — and a row that misnames its own subject sends the next session hunting a function that does not exist. 📏 **FIRST REAL MEASUREMENT: 549 branch tokens** (if 147 · && 171 · || 42 · ?? 37 · ternary 152), comments stripped. Nobody had measured the margin before. 🔑 **AND THE PAIN IS NOT THE SIZE — IT IS THE SURPRISE.** TS2563 counts one reference's flow joins, so it lands in whichever PR adds the last few branches, as a red CI naming a file that PR never touched (twice in one day: Q-978, then Q-975 on #3692). **`tests/theChatHandlerHasHeadroom.test.ts` now fails FIRST, and its message says what to do** — put the condition in a module function, the pattern every TS2563-aware module already follows — so a mystery becomes an instruction. The budget is a **ratchet: it may only go DOWN** (lower it when a block moves out; never raise it to make a PR pass), and it is honestly labelled a PROXY, not the compiler's threshold. **Reversion proven:** one harmless branch added inside the handler → 2 tests fail with the instruction. **STILL OPEN:** the row's own prescription stands — headroom, not a mass rewrite; move self-contained blocks out one PR at a time. This change buys none of that headroom, it only makes the wall visible before anyone walks into it. 📐 **AND THE SHAPE OF THE PROBLEM, MEASURED — nobody had written it down.** The handler is NOT 17,595 lines of evenly-spread logic: it is ~3,600 lines of setup plus **ONE `try` block at raw 13860 that holds 13,933 lines**. Of its 55 depth-1 blocks, the next biggest is 76 branches / 225 lines. So every candidate the row names lives INSIDE that one try, and its 217 children rank like this (branch tokens · lines · raw line): **(1) 178 · 967 · 20372** the third-party-limits / ad-serving pass · **(2) 149 · 732 · 18631** `if (fastLaneWouldRun …)` — the row's own "fast-lane setup" · **(3) 136 · 573 · 24977** `if (result.ok && reviewHeadroomOk && … bugHunt.runReview)` — the reviewer / bug-hunt finishing pass · **(4) 126 · 768 · 15051** the own-repo working-branch (GitHub) storage pass · **(5) 35 · 156 · 26501** the ₹0-bill rule for a build that wrote nothing. **Take them in that order, one PR each, and (5) LAST or never — it is a money rule, and the first absolute rule outranks tidiness.** Each PR proves itself by LOWERING `BRANCH_BUDGET` in `theChatHandlerHasHeadroom` with the full suite green, which is the first time this work has had a measurable finish line. ⛔ **NOT STARTED ON PURPOSE (2026-10-18 01:30).** Extracting a block from the build engine's main handler is a judgement about what it really touches, and a wrong one breaks the core product — while **CI has been failing in 3–4 s all day (Q-1131), so only a local gate could verify it.** Safeguard #3 and the 60-second rule's "safe ambitious path, never a reckless one" both say wait for the real pipeline. The measurement above is the part that costs nothing and makes the refactor fast and safe when it starts. 📊 **MEASURED 2026-10-20, AND THE BRANCHES CLUSTER — ONE EXTRACTION IS WORTH A QUARTER OF THE BUDGET.** Measured with `theChatHandlerHasHeadroom`'s OWN parser (`stripCodeComments` + its `branchTokens`), the handler is 1,370 stripped lines / **542** branch tokens across **115** top-level statement blocks, and they are nowhere near evenly distributed: the top two blocks carry **105** and **90** branch tokens, and the top TEN carry **306 = 56.5%** of the whole budget. **The best single extraction is `landImportedProject`** — the local async helper at `routes/agentv3.ts:11726`, 689 raw lines / 374 stripped / **131 branch tokens = 24.2% of the budget in ONE PR**. It is also the SAFEST, because it already has an explicit parameter contract (`importedFiles`, `opts`, with the diagnostics callbacks injected rather than closed over), and its closure set is small: measured with the TypeScript AST, 26 distinct free identifiers of which 11 are globals (`Date`, `Object`, `Math`, …), leaving roughly **13 real handler-scope dependencies** — `workspaceId` (24 uses), `actuator` (18), `framework` (10), `emit` (7), `userId`, `attachmentContext`, `planningAttachmentText`, `importDurableSaves`, `emitFrameworkCorrection`, `isEditMode`, `isImportTurn`, `events`, `importPreviewBoot`. ⚠️ **The one hazard to design for:** the body ASSIGNS to some of those (`framework` is a handler `let` and the import path corrects it; `planningAttachmentText` likewise), so the extracted function must RETURN those corrections or take explicit setters — a plain read-only options object would silently drop them, which is the 'fix one problem, create problem X' failure this repo bans. 🔴 **AND THIS ROW PREVIOUSLY RECORDED THE OPPOSITE, FROM A MEASUREMENT THAT ANSWERED THE WRONG QUESTION.** An earlier pass this same day measured branch density per 250-LINE WINDOW, got an even 34–67 per window, and concluded there was 'no hot region and no single block whose extraction buys meaningful headroom' — so the row read as though headroom needed MANY PRs. That is false, and the reason is instructive: a window average spreads a dense block across the sparse lines beside it. **Extraction acts on BLOCKS, so the measurement has to be per block.** (An even earlier pass that day counted 3,172 branches — a 5.8x overcount — because an ad-hoc comment strip silently failed and it measured prose as code.) Three wrong numbers in one day, one lesson: **measure the unit you are going to act on, with the guard's own parser — never a proxy and never a re-implementation.** **MEASUREMENT FOOTNOTE:** summing branch tokens per LINE gives 492 where the whole-body scan gives 542, because some ternaries span lines; the budget uses the whole-body figure. **THE SERIES, in payoff order:** (1) `landImportedProject` (131, 24.2%); (2) the weak-admission / scope-blocked block at `:11237` (90, 16.6%); (3) the next eight blocks together (~85). Each PR: pure move, no behaviour change, explicit boundary, full gate on the final state, then lower `BRANCH_BUDGET` to the NEW measurement taken with the guard's parser — down-only, never raised to make a PR pass. ⛔ **AND THE FIRST EXTRACTION MUST NOT START YET, for a reason that is specific and checkable:** on 2026-10-20 PR **#3859** and PR **#3862** are both OPEN and both change `src/server/routes/agentv3.ts`. CLAUDE.md's concurrent-sessions rule is explicit — *do not 'fix' another session's file while it is mid-flight; two sessions editing the same region produce a conflict whoever is right* — and a 689-line move is the largest possible collision. So the measurement above is the deliverable for now; the move itself waits until those two PRs land (or are closed), and then runs against a fresh `main`, re-measuring with the guard's parser first because the numbers above will have shifted by whatever those PRs add. |

| Q-1012 | Found fixing Q-998 | Counting a provider stall that OUR OWN per-turn timeout ended as `PROVIDER_TIME_WASTED` | 🟡 BLOCKED — REQUIRES A DECISION (the admin's) | — | 🔴 **INVESTIGATED, AND THE ROW'S PRESCRIBED FIX COLLIDES WITH A STANDING LESSON — so this is a decision, not a task.** **What is blocked:** counting a provider stall that OUR per-turn timeout ended as `PROVIDER_TIME_WASTED`. **Why:** `providerWaste.ts`'s own header states the opposite rule, learned from autopsy bb688add — *"OUR OWN CLOCK ENDING IS NOT A PROVIDER'S WASTE … a lane that ran out of ITS budget mid-call is our accounting, not the vendor's failure"* — and the per-turn timeout **is** our clock. Recording it as vendor waste would re-introduce exactly what that autopsy removed, and the waste number is what the 429 bench's trigger will be re-chosen from, so inflating it would move a routing decision on a false reading. The honest distinction is whether the provider was PRODUCING when our clock fired (`crawl`/`silent` are real vendor waste; a healthy call our budget ended is not) — and that fact is **not available at the timeout site**: there is no `TurnResult`, so no `firstOutputAfterMs`/`openedAfterMs`, and the chain does not report which rung was serving when a stop cancels it. **What is required:** (a) the admin's call on whether a per-turn-timeout stall counts as vendor waste at all, and (b) if yes, a change INSIDE the provider chain so a cancelled call reports its serving rung and whether it had produced a byte — which CLAUDE.md requires confirming before touching the routing/provider layer. **Already tried:** traced all three `onLlmCall` emitters, `recordWaste`'s callers, `withDeadline`, and the per-turn timeout branch in AgentRunner (`turnTimeoutSignal`); the timeout is correctly re-worded there as a stall rather than a Stop (a sibling already fixed), but it carries no rung and no produced-bytes fact. **Recommendation:** count it only when the chain can say the provider produced nothing — i.e. as `silent`, never as `timeout` — so our own budget ending a healthy answer is still not blamed on a vendor. Not built, because guessing it would put a number nobody measured into the figure a routing decision is made from. **The row's "rare" note holds:** the providers' own 60–120 s timeouts normally fire first. |
| Q-1014 | Found fixing Q-1000 | Platform shell writes (`actuator.runCommand` — the tsc gate's `printf > tsconfig.json`, `rm -f style.css`) never reach the write choke point, so no ledger (POST_GREEN_WRITES, the freeze) sees them | 🟡 BLOCKED — the measurement is done; the remaining change is a live-behaviour decision (its CI blocker lifted when Q-1082 was cleared, 2026-10-15) | — | **MEASURED 2026-10-15, and the measurement narrows this row a lot — so it is written down rather than left as a one-line suspicion.** Census of the route: **50** `actuator.runCommand` sites, of which ~20 carry write-shaped text, but most of those are probes (a `curl` with `>`, a log tail into `/tmp`). The genuinely-writing ones divide three ways: (1) **infra / cache / marker paths the ledger deliberately ignores** — `MARK_ASSET_BASELINE_COMMAND` is a `touch` of a marker, `writeTypecheckCommand` writes a tsbuildinfo cache, `TSC_ENSURE` writes `/tmp` and may install into `node_modules`; `isInfraPath` excludes exactly these from the freeze's concern by design; (2) **app-source removals that DO their own bookkeeping immediately after** — L17723's `rm -f ${extras}` is followed by `writtenFiles.delete(f)`, and L13435 / L25880's removals run inside `runInPass('vaf-remove')` / `('green-guard-remove')` and are followed by `reconcileCapturedWrites`; (3) **the genuinely uncompensated case, which is essentially L21923's `rm -f 'style.css'`** — and even that is followed by a `readFile` that verifies the removal. So the row's class is right (*a write path outside the one choke point*) but the user-visible impact is far smaller than "no ledger sees them" suggests. 🔴 **AND THE OBVIOUS FIX DOES NOT WORK — recorded so the next session does not spend a turn on it.** `inPlatformPass` looks like the answer and is not: a pass NAME only helps writes that pass through the choke point (`actuator.writeFile` → `assertWriteAllowed` / the observer). A shell write never touches `writeFile`, so the name has nothing to attach to — no observer fires and the freeze is never consulted. The real fix is the row's own second option: feed `shellWriteTargets(command)` / `shellRemovalTargets(command)` through the same choke point for PLATFORM commands, exactly as `ToolDispatcher:5526` already does for the model's bash. 🟡 **WHY IT IS NOT DONE HERE, in two parts.** (a) **The ledger half** (record the targets) is safe, but it edits `routes/agentv3.ts` at several sites — the file that sits ON TypeScript's control-flow limit, which raised TS2563 three times on 2026-10-14 — and **CI cannot run** (Q-1082), so a change there would be unverifiable beyond my local gate. (b) **The freeze half** (actually refusing them) is a LIVE-BEHAVIOUR change: routing platform shell writes through `assertWriteAllowed` would make `rm -f style.css` start throwing on a latched app, where today it succeeds. That is the forbidden trade — fixing A while creating X — so it needs CI and, for the user-visible part, the admin. **Needs:** CI restored (Q-1082), then the ledger half alone; the freeze half separately, with the admin. **Tried:** the full call-site census above, reading `inPlatformPass`, `writeRefused`, `isInfraPath` and `shellWriteTargets`'s existing use. **What is blocked:** the live-behaviour change itself; the measurement that would justify it is already done. |
| Q-1130 | Found fixing Q-1023 (2026-10-16) | A bullet longer than `MAX_ITEM_WORDS` (5) counts as **zero** features, so a user who writes DETAILED bullets can be sized as having stated no scope | 🟡 BLOCKED (a routing-and-charging decision reserved to the admin) | — | **MEASURED 2026-10-17 — and the measurement argues AGAINST the change.** The row asked for exactly this before deciding: *"measure how often real prompts lose features this way."* Done, against **all 13 real prompt fixtures** in `tests/fixtures/*prompt*.txt` via `countEnumeratedFeatures`. **What is blocked:** whether `countEnumeratedFeatures` should count a long bullet at all. **Why:** the counter feeds `statesAScope` → complexity routing → which lane a real build takes and what the user is charged. That is a money-and-behaviour decision, which the sixth rule reserves to the admin — not something a session settles inside a wording fix. **What is required:** the admin picks one of the three options below. **Already tried / measured:** the corpus holds ~104 marker lines over the 5-word limit, and the overwhelming majority are **not features** — build instructions (*"Do NOT create src/main.tsx, package.json, or multiple files"*), styling notes (*"Keep the background elegant and non-distracting"*), writing instructions, and pasted assistant advice. **(A) Blanket — any long bullet counts 1:** three fixtures cross `SPEC_FEATURE_COUNT` (14), and two of them are `autopsy8e124182` (7 → **33**) and `report0311186f` (9 → **20**) — the exact autopsies that exist BECAUSE of over-counting. This option REVERSES two shipped fixes. **(B) Head-before-a-gloss — the name before a `:` / em-dash / parenthetical counts 1:** far cleaner, and correctly ignores instruction and styling lines, which carry no gloss separator. Recovers real module names (`autopsy241215d1` 1 → **8**, `autopsyA2b9c802` 6 → 8, `autopsy8e124182` 7 → 9). But it still pushes `report0311186f` 9 → **18**, over the spec line, and the parenthetical branch produced a false positive (`"getDownloadURL()"`). Restricting it to colon/em-dash only is safe on every fixture but barely helps (`autopsy241215d1` only 1 → 3). **(C) Leave the counter alone — RECOMMENDED.** The harm is real but narrow: **1 of 13** fixtures (`autopsy241215d1`, a detailed finance-simulator spec read as 1 feature, so no scope at all). Every measured fix buys that one case by re-creating an over-count elsewhere, and Q-1023's sentence already tells the user that a long bullet does not count. Raw measurement is reproducible from the fixtures with `countEnumeratedFeatures`; the measuring script was deliberately NOT committed (it is scratch, not a gate). |

(Written down 2026-10-17, Q-1126 batch 2: the row already carried What, Needs and Tried, and its reason existed only in prose — which is exactly how the old detector credited it with a `Why` field it did not have.) ✅ **FIRST, MY OWN SUSPICION IN THIS ROW IS DISPROVED BY MEASUREMENT, and the row says so rather than leaving it to stand.** I wrote: *"if the waste meter does not count a crawl-abandonment, this cost is invisible to every future autopsy"*. It is not. Build f9066601's `providerWaste` records **`silent: 2`, `30012 ms`** — exactly the two 15-second abandonments. It files them under `silent` and not `crawl`, and that is CORRECT and documented: `providerNeverAnswered` is tested first, because (turnDeadline.ts) *"The silence is the fact about the provider; how we stopped waiting is ours."* The report's own line states all three facts — timed out, abandoned for crawling, had not started answering — so nothing is hidden and nothing needs fixing there. My earlier reading of `crawl: 0` as a missing measurement was the mistake, and it was the same mistake twice: build 413d29a1's `crawl: 0` is simply true, because that build had no crawl. 🟡 **WHAT REMAINS IS A ROUTING DECISION, WHICH IS THE ADMIN'S.** The real cost stands: 30 seconds of a user's wall-clock before any work began, paid by the cheap opener's crawl bound (15 s) across two rungs. Shortening that bound, or racing the second rung instead of serialising it, is a change to the ladder — and `cheapBuildFloorRunners` carries six separate verbatim admin routing decisions with env overrides that always win, so CLAUDE.md says confirm first (the same reason Q-1042's routing half is blocked). **Recommendation:** keep the ladder exactly as it is and cut the FIRST rung's silent-open bound only — a provider that has sent nothing after ~6 s has not started, and the evidence for that is already measured per call (`SILENT_OPEN_MARK` fires on it). That buys ~18 s of the 30 with no change to which engines run or in what order. **Needs:** the admin's yes. **Tried:** measured both builds' `providerWaste`, read the silent/crawl discriminator and the crawl bench, and confirmed the meter is honest — so there is nothing to fix here without a routing decision. |
| Q-1120 | Report 67a2b0a2 (data-analyst portfolio, Weak/KIMI, 2026-10-15) | A clear 5-part request scored **simple (15)** — and ran 10.1 min, wrote **0 files**, RED, not charged. The first rung (glm-4.7-flashx) sat silent 15 s, the fast lane then **stopped in front of the next rung because it reasons** (`FAST_LANE_FELL_TO_REASONING_RUNG`) and handed its 8-file plan to the full builder — the slowest path (100–140K-char prompts, reasoning engines). It then read `index.css` ×3, `tsconfig.json`, `tsconfig.build.json` and grepped (17–69 s per turn), had two 150 s calls where "only reasoning arrived, no answer began", and the futility breaker ended it at minute 10 | 🟡 BLOCKED — REQUIRES ACTION (routing decision: the admin's) | — | **What:** a simple app should not become a 10-minute reasoning-engine run because one first rung was silent. **Why blocked:** ladders are routing (`ROUTING_AND_BILLING.md`) and the admin's Q-962 "keep" answer (2026-10-11) predates this evidence: **the silent first rung hit BOTH reports of 2026-10-14/15** (398db9f3 and 67a2b0a2). **Options:** (a) the fast lane skips reasoning rungs to the first DIRECT-answering rung (Haiku, already on the Weak ladder; its call is a few hundred tokens, a fraction of a rupee) — recommended; (b) keep as is; (c) a cross-build bench so the 15 s is paid once per window, not per build. **Needs:** admin pick of a/b/c. **Tried:** read the whole timeline and the fast-lane handoff reasons; the lane code was not changed because it is the ladder. |
| Q-1121 | Report 67a2b0a2 | Two reasoning engines in a row burned their whole 150 s timeout on "only reasoning arrived, no answer began" (KIMI at +339 s, GLM-5.3 at +489 s), then a third call hung 110 s more: 315 s = 52% of the clock in calls that answered nothing | 🟡 BLOCKED — REQUIRES ACTION (missing information) | — | **What:** the per-call timeout floor (150 s) is the only bound on a reasoning-only call. **Why blocked:** lowering it would also kill legitimately slow answers; the data to choose a number (`llmCalls[].openMs / firstOutputMs`, recorded since 303e8a6f) is still thin. **Needs:** ~1 week of those fields across Weak reports, then pick the bound. **Tried:** read the call table; the engine-time verdict (this PR) at least stops the summary blaming the request. |
| Q-1122 | Report 67a2b0a2 | The futility breaker waited its whole 10-minute window while the build had written nothing and 52% of the clock was provider failures | 🟡 BLOCKED — REQUIRES ACTION (policy: the admin's) | — | **What:** a person waited 10 min for nothing. **Why blocked:** `AGENTV3_FUTILITY_MINUTES` (default 10) is a user-visible policy: shorter ends slow-but-working builds. **Options:** (a) 6 min when zero files AND zero commands AND the engine-time verdict is true (this PR's `engineTimeDominated`) — recommended, because it only shortens a build the ledger already proves is engine-starved; (b) keep 10. **Needs:** admin pick. **Tried:** read `futilityBreaker.ts` wiring; the env override exists but is global. |

| Q-2202 | Found 2026-10-20 migrating the Q-886 census (#3865) — not reported by anyone | **109 test files hand-roll the same recursive source walk; only 3 use the shared helper, and a private copy throws ENOENT on a generated fixture that vanishes mid-walk** | 🟡 BLOCKED — REQUIRES ACTION (it is another session’s in-flight centralisation) | — | **What is blocked:** migrating the remaining 109 files onto `tests/helpers/sourceCensus.ts`. **Why:** that helper arrived hours ago in **#3858, from another session**, which migrated three files. A 109-file mechanical sweep of someone else’s in-flight centralisation is the race the concurrency rules forbid — and the change is not as mechanical as the count suggests: every one of those files carries its own filter and ignore list, so a careless sweep silently widens or narrows what each census sees, which is worse than the fragility it fixes. **What is required to unblock:** either that session finishing (then this row closes itself and is verified by re-measuring), or the admin saying this session should take it over — at which point it is done in small batches with each census’s own filter preserved and re-measured, never one sweep. **Already tried / measured:** 130 test files reference `readdirSync`; **109** of them self-recurse with `statSync(...).isDirectory()`; 3 use the helper (`unusedDependencies`, `aStarvedReadSaysSoAndIsRead`, and the isolation test itself). The failure is REAL, not theoretical — with a `.tmp-*` dangling symlink under `src/server`, a hand-rolled walk throws `ENOENT` while the migrated census passes all 11 of its tests. One file is already migrated in #3865: the census this session wrote, which is the one it owns. |
| Q-2203 | Found 2026-10-20 landing #3859 (CI run11842) — not reported by anyone | ⏱️ **A test asserts a timer was RELEASED at a point that does not await the release, so it is load-sensitive: `tests/queueRouteCompletion.test.ts:166` expects `vi.getTimerCount()` to be 0 immediately after `await request` on the build-authority refusal path, and CI read 1** | 🟡 BLOCKED — REQUIRES ACTION (missing information: one occurrence, not reproducible) | — | **What:** confirm whether the handler can resolve its request promise while its 1-second ping timer is still pending, and if so make the cleanup part of what the promise awaits. **Why blocked:** ONE CI failure against SIX local passes — the file alone 3x (15 passed), the file alone under `--coverage` 2x, and the whole suite on the identical commit `c7e827aab` (40,776 passed); the fourth gate of #3859 then ran CI's own `npm run test:coverage` over the full suite (40,802 passed) and it did not reproduce either. A race that will not reproduce cannot be root-caused honestly, and guessing at the fix would be a surface patch on the build route. **Needs:** a second occurrence, or a full-suite-under-contention reproduction. **Already tried:** the six runs above, plus a probe that advanced 60 extra microtasks after `await request` and saw the count at 0 on the FIRST read locally. **MEASURED FACTS, so the next session does not re-derive them:** the assertion is UNCHANGED by #3859 — it is at `:96` on `main` and `:166` on that branch, moved down only because 74 lines of new tests were inserted above it, and the diff contains no +/- inside its block; `main`'s own CI is green on it. **CLASS:** asserting a resource has been released at a point that does not await the release. **SIBLINGS HUNTED (13 `getTimerCount()` assertions in 8 files) AND ELEVEN ARE SOUND, which is the point of hunting them:** `deploymentRegistry.test.ts:76` awaits the rejection that clears the deadline and `buildSourcePublication.ts:53` awaits `joined`, so the clearing is inside the awaited work. Only this one awaits a REQUEST whose cleanup is registered to run after the response. Remaining sites: `buildSourcePublication.test.ts:31,53` · `buildDeadlineLifecycle.test.ts:251` · `buildRequestCoordination.test.ts:28` · `deploymentRegistry.test.ts:76,82` · `queuedContinuationClient.test.ts:82,89,97` · `workspaceImportLease.test.ts:43,85` · `zipUpload.test.ts:312`. ⛔ **DO NOT widen or delete the assertion, and do not quarantine the test** — CLAUDE.md forbids both, and the assertion protects a real property: the handler must not leak its ping timer on a refusal. The fix is upstream or there is no fix yet.  🔴 **CONFIRMED 2026-10-20, SECOND OCCURRENCE — this row is no longer 'one unreproduced failure', and the evidence now points at a PR rather than at load alone.** The same assertion failed again at the same line with the same value (`expected 1 to be +0`) on a DIFFERENT commit and run: first `c7e827aab` (run11842), then `99e13b200` (run11866). CLAUDE.md's own rule is that a second failure is real, so the flake reading is retired. **AND THE DISTRIBUTION IS THE DIAGNOSIS:** it has failed TWICE on `codex/phase-2-queue-handoff` (#3859) and ZERO times anywhere else — `main` is green on this test throughout (run11858 on `94c4d7d84`), and seven local runs passed (the file alone 3x, under `--coverage` 2x, the whole suite twice, once via CI's own `npm run test:coverage` at 40,802 passed). The failing path is `BUILD_AUTHORITY_UNAVAILABLE`, and `src/server/AgentV3/BuildRequestCoordination.ts` is one of only TWO files where #3859 and `main` genuinely overlap (measured from their merge base: 94 files changed on main, 36 on the branch, overlap = that file plus `PROGRESS.md`); #3859 also rewrites `runningBuildRegistry.ts` and `LiveChannel.ts` on the same refusal path. **So the reading is that #3859's refusal path leaves a timer un-cleared and this pre-existing `main` test is correctly catching it** — the test is doing its job, and #3859 cannot reach green until its author clears that timer. Five merge-and-gate cycles from another session cannot fix a defect inside its own diff. **NOT pushed into their logic on purpose:** a larger ask on a PR this session did not open goes to the author with a proposal, which is the fix already named in this row — make the handler's cleanup part of what the request promise awaits, so 'response sent' and 'timers cleared' cannot be observed out of order. That kills the class, not just this branch. |
